, and the geodesic flow is completely dissipative with respect t
m_{\Gamma}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
10.6
, we have the dichotomy that either
\mu_{o}(L^{\text{rad}}_{\Gamma})=\mu_{o}({\partial X})
or
\mu_{o}(L^{\text{rad}}_{\Gamma})=0
. If
\mu_{o}(L^{\text{rad}}_{\Gamma})=\mu_{o}({\partial X})
, by the local product structure of
m_{\Gamma}
and Lemma
10.8
, we have
\Omega_{C}
has full
m_{\Gamma}
-measure in
SM
and hence
\phi^{t}
is completely conservative. Analogous arguments hold for the latter case and
\phi^{t}
is completely dissipative.
∎

The rest of this subsection are devoted to proving the following.

Proposition 10.10
.

If
\Gamma
is nonelementary and contains an expansive isometry, then
\mu_{o}(L^{\text{rad}}_{\Gamma})=0

implies that
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}
converges.

We first define for
x\in X
and
c>0

(35)

K^{0}(x,c):=\{v\in SX:d(x,\pi v)<c\text{\ and\ }x\in H(v)\}.

We note that in
[
76
]
for the nonpositive curvature case,
K^{0}(x,c)
is defined using a vector
v(x;v^{-},v^{+})
. More precisely, for a point
x\in X
and
(\xi,\eta)\in\partial^{2}X
we denote
v(x;\xi,\eta)
the unique element
v\in SX
whose footpoint is the orthogonal projection of
x
to the flat strip
(\xi\eta)
and such that
P(v)=(\xi,\eta)
. This is well defined in nonpositive curvature case. In our setting, due to the possible existence of focal points,
v(x;\xi,\eta)
is not uniquely defined. Our new definition in (
35
) does not affect anything, in particular the proof of Lemma
10.11
below still works.

We need to thicken these sets by the geodesic flow and consider

K(x,c):=\{\phi^{s}v:v\in K^{0}(x,c),s\in(-c,c)\}\subset SB(x,2c).

Such a set has the important property that the orbit of an arbitrary vector
v\in SX
under the geodesic flow either does not intersect it at all or spends precisely time
2c
inside.

Lemma 10.11
.

P(\{K(o,c)\cap\phi^{-t}\gamma K(\gamma o,c):t>0\})={\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)
.

Proof.

The proof of
[
76
, Lemma 6.2]
still works here under our new definition (
35
).
∎

Recall that
h\in\Gamma
is an expansive isometry with fixed points
h^{-},h^{+}\in{\partial X}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
o\in Ax(h)
. We fix
\epsilon>0
and the associated neighborhoods
U=U_{\epsilon},V=V_{\epsilon}\subset\overline{X}
of
h^{-},h^{+}
respectively provided by Proposition
7.7
. Let
c_{0}>r
be the two constants from the shadow lemma Proposition
10.4
. Let
\Lambda\subset\Gamma
be a finite symmetric set provided by Proposition
10.1
and

\rho:=\{d(o,\beta o):\beta\in\Lambda\}.

Fix
c>c_{0}+\rho+\epsilon>r+\rho+\epsilon
and from now on consider the set

K:=\overline{K(o,c)}.

Lemma 10.12
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for
T
sufficiently large,

\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{0}^{T}ds\sum_{\gamma,\phi\in\Gamma}m(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)\leq C\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq T}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}\right)^{2}.

Proof.

Recall the definition of measure
\lambda_{\xi,\eta}
. It is relatively easier to get a upper bounded for it. If

(\xi,\eta)\in P(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)

for some
s,t>0
, then

\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{0}^{T}ds\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)\leq(2c)^{4}.

By Lemma
10.11
,

P(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{2c,2c}(o,\phi o)\subset{\partial X}\times{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{2c,2c}(o,\phi o).

Therefor for any
\gamma,\phi\in\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{0}^{T}dsm(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16c^{4}\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{2c,2c}(o,\phi o)}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)e^{2\delta_{\Gamma}\beta_{o}(\xi,\eta)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16c^{4}e^{4c\delta_{\Gamma}}\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{2c,2c}(o,\phi o)}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16c^{4}e^{4c\delta_{\Gamma}}\int_{{\partial X}}d\mu_{o}(\xi)\int_{{\mathcal{O}}^{+}_{2c,2c}(o,\phi o)}d\mu_{o}(\eta)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16c^{4}e^{4c\delta_{\Gamma}}\mu_{o}({\partial X})D(2c)e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\phi o)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\phi o)}

where
C_{1}:=16c^{4}e^{4c\delta_{\Gamma}}\mu_{o}({\partial X})D(2c)
only depends on
c
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{0}^{T}ds\sum_{\gamma,\phi\in\Gamma}m(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K\cap g^{-t-s}\phi K)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{\gamma,\phi\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq T+4c,d(\gamma o,\phi o)\leq T+4c}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}(d(o,\gamma o)+d(\gamma o,\phi o)-12c)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{1}e^{12c\delta_{\Gamma}}\sum_{\gamma,\phi\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq T+4c,d(o,\alpha o)\leq T+4c}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}(d(o,\gamma o)+d(o,\alpha o))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\left(\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq T+4c}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}\right)^{2}

where
C_{2}
is a constant only depending on
c
. Finally, notice that

\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,T<d(o,\gamma o)\leq T+4c}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}

is uniformly bounded in
T
. This follows from the proof of
[
73
, Corollary 3.8]
, which essentially uses the shadow lemma Proposition
10.4
. We are done.
∎

Lemma 10.13
.

There exists a constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that for
T
sufficiently large,

\int_{0}^{T}dt\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}m(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)\geq C^{\prime}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq T}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}.

Proof.

By Lemma
10.11
and non-negativity of Gromov product, we have for
\gamma\in\Gamma
with
d(o,\gamma o)>4c
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dtm(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dt\int_{\partial^{2}X}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)e^{2\delta_{\Gamma}\beta_{o}(\xi,\eta)}\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)\left(\int_{0}^{T}dt\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)\right).

If
(\xi,\eta)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)\cap(U\times V)
, by Proposition
7.7
, any geodesic connecting
\xi
and
\eta
has distance less than
\epsilon
to
o
, and so is the generalized strip
(\xi\eta)
. Since
\lambda_{\xi,\eta}
is supported on the generalized strip
(\xi\eta)
, we have for
T>d(o,\gamma o)+4c
that

\int_{0}^{T}dt\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)=2c^{2}.

Since
\Gamma
acts on
SX
by isometries, the above also holds for
(\xi,\eta)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)\cap\beta(U\times V)
for any
\beta\in\Gamma
. So for any
\beta\in\Lambda
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dtm(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)\cap\beta(U\times V)}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)\left(\int_{0}^{T}dt\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o)\cap\beta(U\times V)}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta).

By Lemma
10.1
, for
\gamma\in\Gamma
satisfying
d(o,\gamma o)>R
, there exists some
\beta\in\Lambda
such that

{\mathcal{L}}_{r,c}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o)\cap(U\times V)\supset(U\cap{\partial X})\times{\mathcal{O}}_{r,c}^{-}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o).

Since
c>r+\rho+\epsilon>r+\rho
, we also have

{\mathcal{L}}_{r,c}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o)=\beta^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{r,c}(\beta o,\gamma o)\subset\beta^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{r+\rho,c}(o,\gamma o)\subset\beta^{-1}{\mathcal{L}}_{c,c}(o,\gamma o).

Thus we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}dtm(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\int_{\beta((U\cap{\partial X})\times{\mathcal{O}}_{r,c}^{-}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o))}d\mu_{o}(\xi)d\mu_{o}(\eta)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\mu_{o}(\beta U)\mu_{o}(\beta{\mathcal{O}}_{r,c}^{-}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\mu_{o}(\beta U)e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\beta^{-1}o)}\mu_{o}({\mathcal{O}}_{r,c}^{-}(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\mu_{o}(\beta U)e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\beta^{-1}o)}D(c)e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\beta^{-1}\gamma o)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2c^{2}\min_{\beta\in\Lambda}\mu_{o}(\beta U)e^{-2\delta_{\Gamma}\rho}D(c)e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}.

Finally, summing over all
\gamma\in\Gamma
with
d(o,\gamma o)\in(R,T-4c)
we get

\int_{0}^{T}dt\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}m(K\cap g^{-t}\gamma K)\geq C^{\prime}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,R<d(o,\gamma o)\leq T-4c}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}.

Notice that both
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,d(o,\gamma o)\leq R}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}
and
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma,T-4c<d(o,\gamma o)\leq T}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}
are uniformly bounded. We are done.
∎

Finally, we can prove Proposition
10.10
.

Proof of Proposition
10.10
.

If
\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{-\delta_{\Gamma}d(o,\gamma o)}
diverges, by Lemmas
10.12
and
10.13
, we can apply the second Borel-Cantelli lemma and proceeds as in
[
102
, p. 20]
to get

m_{\Gamma}(\{v\in SM:\int_{0}^{\infty}\chi_{K_{\Gamma}\cap\phi^{-t}K_{\Gamma}}(v)dt=\infty\})>0

where
K_{\Gamma}\subset SM
is the projection of
K
onto
SM
. It means that
(SM,\phi^{t},m_{\Gamma})
is not completely dissipative. However, by Proposition
10.9
,
\mu_{o}(L^{\text{rad}}_{\Gamma})=0
implies that it is completely dissipative. A contradiction and we are done.
∎

10.4.
Ergodicity for divergent groups

Theorem 10.14

(Hopf’s individual ergodic theorem, cf.
[
55
]
)
.

Assume that
(\Omega,\phi^{t},\mu)
is conservative, and let
\rho\in L^{1}(\mu)
be a function which is strictly positive
\mu
-almost
everywhere. Then for any function
f\in L^{1}(\mu)
, the limits

f^{\pm}(\omega)=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{T}f(\phi^{\pm t}(\omega))dt}{\int_{0}^{T}\rho(\phi^{\pm t}(\omega))dt}

exist and are equal for
\mu\text{-a.e.}\ \omega\in\Omega
. Moreover,

(1)

the functions
f^{+},f^{-}
are measurable and flow invariant,

(2)

\rho\cdot f^{+},\rho\cdot f^{-}\in L^{1}(\mu)
,

(3)

for every bounded measurable flow-invariant function
h
we have

\int_{\Omega}\rho(\omega)f^{\pm}(\omega)h(\omega)dM(\omega)=\int_{\Omega}f(\omega)h(\omega)d\mu(\omega),

(4)

(\Omega,\phi^{t},\mu)
is ergodic if and only if for every
f\in L^{1}(\mu)
,
f^{+}
is constant
\mu
-almost
everywhere.

The following lemma is crucial to apply Hopf argument.

Lemma 10.15
.

Let
v\in\mathcal{R}_{0}\subset SX
be a recurrent vector. Then for any
w\in{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(v)\subset SX
, there exist a sequence
\{\alpha_{n}\}^{\infty}_{n=1}\subset\Gamma
and a sequence of time
\{t_{n}\}^{\infty}_{n=1}
with
t_{n}\rightarrow+\infty
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}d\alpha_{n}\phi^{t_{n}}v=v
and

\lim_{n\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{t_{n}}v)=0.

If moreover
X
has bounded asymptote, then

\lim_{t\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t}w,\phi^{t}v)=0.

Similar results hold for negatively asymptotic vectors.

Proof.

Since
v
is a recurrent vector in
SX
, there exist a sequence
\{\alpha_{n}\}^{\infty}_{n=1}\subset\Gamma
and a sequence of time
\{t_{n}\}^{\infty}_{n=1}
with
t_{n}\rightarrow+\infty
, such that

(36)

d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}v)\to v,~~~n\to+\infty.

Assume the contrary that there exists
a>0
such that

\liminf_{n\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{t_{n}}v)>a.

By taking a subsequence, let us assume that
\lim_{n\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{t_{n}}v)>a.

Since
w\in{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(v)
,
d(\phi^{t}(w),\phi^{t}(v))\leq D,\forall t\geq 0
for some
D>0
.
Then for each
t_{n}
in (
36
), we have for any
s\in[-t_{n},+\infty)
,

(37)

\displaystyle d(\phi^{t_{n}+s}(w),\phi^{t_{n}+s}(v))=d(d\alpha_{n}\circ\phi^{t_{n}+s}w,d\alpha_{n}\circ\phi^{t_{n}+s}v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d(\phi^{s}\circ d\alpha_{n}\circ\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{s}\circ d\alpha_{n}\circ\phi^{t_{n}}v).

Note that
d(\phi^{t_{n}}(w),\phi^{t_{n}}(v))\leq D
, and
d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}v)\rightarrow v
.
So we know that for any
\epsilon>0
there is an integer
N>0
such that for all
n>N
,

d(v,d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}w))\leq D+\epsilon.

This implies that the set
\{d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}w)\}
has an accumulate point. Without loss of generality, we assume
d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}w)\rightarrow w^{\prime}
. Letting
n\rightarrow+\infty
in (
37
), we get that

d(\phi^{s}w^{\prime},\phi^{s}v)\leq D,~~~\forall~s\in\mathbb{R}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle d(w^{\prime},v)=\lim_{n\to\infty}d(d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}w),d\alpha_{n}(\phi^{t_{n}}v))=\lim_{n\to\infty}d(\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{t_{n}}v)>a.

Thus
w^{\prime}\neq v
. It follows that the geodesics
c_{v}
and
c_{w^{\prime}}
bound a generalized strip,
which contradicts to the assumption that
v\in\mathcal{R}_{0}
.

Assume that
X
has bounded asymptote. Since
w\in{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(v)
, there exists
C>0
such that for any
0<t_{n}<t
,

d(\phi^{t}w,\phi^{t}v)\leq Cd(\phi^{t_{n}}w,\phi^{t_{n}}v).

Thus
\lim_{t\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t}w,\phi^{t}v)=0.
We are done with the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.16
.

The function

\tilde{\rho}:SX\to{\mathbb{R}}
by

\tilde{\rho}(u):=e^{-4\delta_{\Gamma}d(\pi u,\Gamma o)},\ \forall u\in SX

descends to a function
\rho:SM\to{\mathbb{R}}
and belongs to
L^{1}(m_{\Gamma})
. Moreover, for any
u,v\in SX
with
d(\pi u,\pi v)\leq 1
,

(38)

|\tilde{\rho}(u)-\tilde{\rho}(v)|\leq\tilde{\rho}(u)4\delta_{\Gamma}d(\pi u,\pi v)e^{4\delta_{\Gamma}}.

Proof.

We only need to verify
\rho\in L^{1}(m_{\Gamma})
since other statements are straightforward. For any
(\xi,\eta)\in\partial^{2}X
and
R\geq 1
,

\lambda_{\xi,\eta}(P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap SB(o,R))\leq 2R.

If
P^{-1}(\xi,\eta)\cap SB(o,R)\neq\emptyset
, then
\beta_{o}(\xi,\eta)\leq 2R
. Thus

m(SB(o,R))\leq 2Re^{2\delta_{\Gamma}R}.

Let
D_{\Gamma}
denote the Dirichlet domain for
\Gamma
with center
o
. Then
\tilde{\rho}(u)=e^{-4\delta_{\Gamma}d(\pi u,o)}
for all
u\in S\overline{D_{\Gamma}}
. Denote

W(R):=SB(o,R)\setminus SB(o,R-1)\cap S\overline{D_{\Gamma}}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{W(R)}\tilde{\rho}(u)dm(u)\leq e^{-4\delta_{\Gamma}(R-1)}\int_{SB(o,R)}dm(u)\leq CRe^{-2\delta_{\Gamma}R}\leq Ce^{-\delta_{\Gamma}R}

for some constant
C>0
. Hence
\rho\in L^{1}(m_{\Gamma})
.
∎

Proposition 10.17
.

Assume that
X
has bounded asymptote and
\Gamma
contains a regular isometry
h
. If
(SM,(\phi^{t})_{t\in{\mathbb{R}}},m_{\Gamma})
is completely conservative, then it is ergodic.

Proof.

Let
f\in C_{c}(SM,{\mathbb{R}})
and
\tilde{f}\in C(SX,{\mathbb{R}})
be a lift to
SX
. Let
K=\mathop{\hbox{{\rm supp}}}(f)\subset SM
. Since
(SM,(\phi^{t})_{t\in{\mathbb{R}}},m_{\Gamma})
is completely conservative, Hopf’s individual ergodic theorem states that for
m\text{-a.e.}\ u\in SX
, the limits

\tilde{f}^{\pm}(u)=\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{f}(\phi^{\pm t}(u))dt}{\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{\rho}(\phi^{\pm t}(u))dt}

exist and are equal.

Let
h^{+},h^{-}
denote the attractive and repulsive fixed point of the regular isometry
h\in\Gamma
. By Proposition
7.10
, there exist open neighborhoods
U
of
h^{-}
and
V
of
h^{+}
in
X
such that each
\xi\in U
and
\eta\in V
can be joined by a unique regular geodesic
c_{\xi,\eta}
. We define

\displaystyle\Omega(U,V):=

\displaystyle\{w\in SX:w^{-}\in U,w^{+}\in V\},

\displaystyle\Omega^{\text{rec}}(U,V):=

\displaystyle\{w\in\Omega(U,V):w\text{\ is recurrent}\},

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}(U,V):=

\displaystyle\{w\in\Omega^{\text{rec}}(U,V):\tilde{f}^{+}(u)=\tilde{f}^{-}(u)\}.

Lemma 10.18
.

Let
v\in\Omega^{\text{rec}}(U,V)
and
w\in{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(v)
. Then
\tilde{f}^{+}(w)=\tilde{f}^{+}(v)
. Analogous statement holds for
{\mathcal{F}}^{u}
and
\tilde{f}^{-}
.

Proof.

We have
v\in\mathcal{R}_{1}
by Proposition
7.10
. In fact by Lemma
8.1
,
w\in\mathcal{R}_{1}
. By Lemma
10.15
,

\lim_{t\to+\infty}d(\phi^{t}w,\phi^{t}v)=0.

Then the conclusion of the lemma follows from the continuity of
f
and (
38
) applied to
v
and
w
.
∎

By the local product structure of
m
, there exists a vector
w\in\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)
with
w^{-}=\xi
such that

G_{\xi}:=\{\eta\in V:\exists u\in\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)\text{\ with\ }u^{-}=\xi,u^{+}=\eta\}

has full
\mu_{o}
-measure in
V
. Let us fix such
w
and
\xi
in the following discussion.

Lemma 10.19
.

\tilde{f}^{+}
is constant
m\text{-a.e.}\
on
\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)
.

Proof.

For
m\text{-a.e.}\ v\in\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)
, we have
v^{+}\in G_{\xi}
. Let
u\in\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)
be such that
u^{-}=\xi=w^{-}
and
u^{+}=v^{+}
. Then there exist
s_{1},s_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\phi^{s_{1}}u\in{\mathcal{F}}^{s}(v)
and
\phi^{s_{2}}u\in{\mathcal{F}}^{u}(w)
. Then by Lemma
10.18
,

\tilde{f}^{+}(v)=\tilde{f}^{+}(\phi^{s_{1}}u)=\tilde{f}^{+}(\phi^{s_{2}}u)=\tilde{f}^{-}(\phi^{s_{2}}u)=\tilde{f}^{-}(w)=\tilde{f}^{+}(w).

∎

Let
V^{\prime}:=\{\eta\in V:\exists u\in\Omega^{\prime}(U,V)\text{\ with\ }u^{+}=\eta\}
. Using Lemma
10.18
again and Lemma
10.19
, we see that
\tilde{f}^{+}
is constant
m\text{-a.e.}\
on

\{v\in SX:v^{+}\in V^{\prime}\}.

Consider the set
Y:=\bigcup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma V^{\prime}
. Let
Z:=\{v\in SX:v^{+}\in Y\}
.

Lemma 10.20
.

Z
has full
m
-measure in
SX
.

Proof.

We have
\mu_{o}(Y\cap V)=\mu_{o}(V)
. By the conformality, we have
\mu_{x}(Y\cap V)=\mu_{x}(V)
for every
x\in X
. For any
\eta\in L_{\Gamma}^{\text{rad}}
, there exists an open neighborhood
O(\eta)\subset{\partial X}
which can be mapped by an element of
\Gamma
to
V
. Thus
\mu_{o}(Y\cap O(\eta))=\mu_{o}(O(\eta))
for each
\eta\in L_{\Gamma}^{\text{rad}}
. So
\mu_{o}(Y)=\mu_{o}(L_{\Gamma}^{\text{rad}})
. It follows that
Z
has full
m
-measure.
∎

It is clear that
\tilde{f}^{+}
is
m\text{-a.e.}\
on
Z
, so it is constant
m\text{-a.e.}\
on
SX
.

Finally, notice that
C_{c}(SM,{\mathbb{R}})
is dense in
L^{1}(m_{\Gamma})
. So
\phi^{t}
is ergodic with respect
m_{\Gamma}
by Hopf’s individual ergodic Theorem
10.14
, which completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Proof of Theorem
I
.

By Proposition
10.9
, we have the dichotomy. In each case, (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(ii) follows from Lemma
10.5
and Proposition
10.10
. (ii)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) follows from Proposition
10.9
.

If furthermore
X
has bounded asymptote and
\Gamma
contains a regular isometry, in the second case, (iii)
\Rightarrow
(iv) follows from Proposition
10.17
and (iv)
\Rightarrow
(iii) is straightforward since
m_{\Gamma}
is not supported on a single orbit.
∎
